assistant: Neither `jsonschema` nor `httpx` is installed or declared, so both are new dependencies for step 7. Remote includes and `!literal` mostly touch the loader. The `file:` prefix is also hard-coded in about six write-back places, listed in (3). All paths below are under `/work/repo`.\n\n## (1) Dependencies (`pyproject.toml`)\n- Runtime: `eventsourcing>=9.5.5,<10` and `ruamel.yaml>=0.19.1,<0.20`.\n- Dev: mypy (strict, over src and tests), pytest, pytest-xdist, ruff.\n- Neither `jsonschema` nor `httpx` appears in `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` or `.venv` site-packages (nor do `requests` or `referencing`).\n\n## (2) Module map (`src/troupe/`)\n**`config/`**\n- `constants.py`: `INCLUDE_PREFIX="file:"` (:15), `INCLUDE_REF_KEY="$ref"` (:18), `YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES` (:27), `CONFIG_DIR_NAME=".troupe"`, poll interval.\n- `errors.py`: `ExitCode` and the `TroupeError` subclasses that carry the exit code.\n- `model.py`: frozen model (`SourceFile`, `Span`, `Origin`, `ConfigScalar/Mapping/Sequence`, `IncludeKind`, `IncludeRef`, `Anchor`, `AliasRef`).\n- `loader.py`: the only reader of config files. Composes YAML, builds the model, expands includes, refuses unsupported YAML.\n- `compose.py`: ruamel composer hook that records alias occurrences.\n- `overrides.py`: `deep_merge` for `$ref` overrides, `is_ref_key`, `override_entries`, `physical_entry`.\n- `merge_keys.py`: merged view for `<<`.\n- `paths.py`: parses dotted PATHs.\n- `resolve.py`: `walk_path` (:61) and `flatten_leaves` (:113).\n- `scalars.py`: YAML 1.2 core-schema typing.\n- `yaml_style.py`: quote and literal-block style rules.\n- `locate.py`: finds `.troupe/troupe.yml|yaml`.\n- `lock.py`: flock on `.troupe/`.\n- `theatre.py`: `open_theatre` (locate, lock, load, store, import or reconcile).\n- `history.py`, `store.py`, `recorded.py`, `lineage.py`: the event-sourced history.\n- `reconcile.py`: diffs file leaves against recorded leaves.\n- `watch.py`: polling watcher.\n\n**`config/writeback/`** (the import chain is in `__init__.py`)\n- `value.py`: parses the `set` VALUE.\n- `compare.py`: plain trees, `IncludeDirective`, `plain_equal`, `splice`.\n- `layout.py`: text layout of a file.\n- `edits.py`: applies text edits.\n- `scalar_render.py`, `block_scalar.py`, `collection_render.py`: rendering.\n- `target.py`: `resolve_target` and the placement walk.\n- `shared.py`: files included at several places; loads new includes from VALUE.\n- `aliases.py`: alias/merge guard. `expand.py`: `--expand-alias`.\n- `planner.py`: plans the patches.\n- `verify.py`: read-back. `leaf_diff.py`: history changes. `atomic.py`: the only writer of user files.\n- `setter.py`: the `set_value` use case.\n\n**`cli/`**\n- `main.py`: parser, registrars (:79), `run` (:101).\n- `shell.py`: shared parser and protocols.\n- `set_command.py`, `get_show_command.py`, `watch_command.py`: subcommands.\n- `render.py`: output formatting, including `source_label` (:82).\n- `yaml_output.py`: output of `show`.\n- `actor.py`: `TROUPE_ACTOR`.\n\n## (3) Where includes are recognised, how they are represented, where tags are refused\n**Recognition**\n- Scalar form: `_Builder._build`, `loader.py:344-345`, checks `isinstance(value, str) and value.startswith(INCLUDE_PREFIX)` on any string value, never a key.\n- Mapping form:\n  - `_build_mapping` checks `is_ref_key(key)` (:408).\n  - `_ref_value` (:485-506) requires the value to be a `file:` string (:494). Otherwise it raises "`$ref` must be an include string (file:...)" (:503).\n  - The include is expanded at :435 with `form=mapping`.\n- Expansion:\n  - `_Builder.include` (:508) and `_include` (:530-590).\n  - Target path is `self._source.path.parent / spec` (:540).\n  - Text vs YAML is decided by `target_path.name.endswith(YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES)` (:541).\n  - A text include becomes a `ConfigScalar(value=target.text, style=None)` with `IncludeRef(IncludeKind.TEXT, ...)` (:549-556).\n  - A YAML include goes through `_refuse_cycle` (:557, :592), then `read_include`, `_compose`, and a recursive `_Builder` with the chain extended (:558-564).\n  - Overrides use `deep_merge` (:578). An empty file gives a null scalar (:579-585). A nested root include is stored as `inner` (:588-590).\n- File reading:\n  - `_Loader.read_include` (:198-221) caches by `real_path`, honours the `overrides` dict, and raises `ConfigLoadError` (exit 4) for a missing or unreadable target.\n  - `_Loader._remember` (:223) creates the `SourceFile` and appends it to `files`.\n- Extra entry point: `load_include(including, spec, overrides)` (:155-169), used by `writeback/shared.py:460` for includes newly written by `set`.\n\n**Representation** (`model.py`)\n- `IncludeKind` (:142) is `NONE`, `YAML` or `TEXT`.\n- `IncludeRef` (:151) holds:\n  - `kind`\n  - `spec`: the text after `file:`\n  - `target: SourceFile`\n  - `directive: Origin`\n  - `form`: the `$ref` mapping, or None for the scalar form\n  - `inner`\n- `SourceFile` (:92) holds `path`, `real_path` (a resolved `Path`, used as identity), `text` and `line_ending`. Everything assumes a local `Path`; a remote include would need a synthetic one.\n\n**Places outside the loader that rebuild the string as `"file:" + spec`** (each needs updating for remote includes)\n- `writeback/compare.py:91` (`node_to_plain`).\n- `writeback/compare.py:138-146` (`_include_text`): a VALUE string starting with `file:` becomes a contentless `IncludeDirective`.\n- `writeback/shared.py:456` (`spec = include.text[len(INCLUDE_PREFIX):]`).\n- `writeback/shared.py:481` (`_is_yaml` uses the suffixes).\n- `writeback/shared.py:523`.\n- `writeback/planner.py:723` (`f"file:{include.spec}"`, used in refusal messages).\n\n**Explicit tags are refused**\n- `_Builder._refuse_properties` (`loader.py:620-635`) raises `ConfigLoadError` "explicit tag '{tag}' is not supported", which exits 4.\n- Tag detection is `_explicit_tag` (:637-653), plus `_tags_next_to_anchors` (:655) for tags next to an anchor.\n- It is called for every node (:341), for keys (:395) and for `$ref` values (:491).\n- VALUE tags are refused separately in `writeback/value.py` `_refuse_properties` (~:139-145) as a `UsageError` (exit 2).\n\n## (4) How the writer picks the target file\n- `setter.set_value` (`setter.py:~73`) runs `resolve_target`, then `_expanded` (alias guard), then the no-op check, then `plan_set`, `verify_patch`, `diff_leaves`, `write_patches`, and finally `record_changes`.\n- `target.resolve_target` (`target.py:163`):\n  - Refuses a `$ref` PATH segment.\n  - Calls `walk_path`, then `_target` (:210). The result is one of:\n    - `ReplaceTarget`: the path exists.\n    - `AddKeyTarget`: a key is missing under a mapping. Its placement is `_place` (:412), the placement walk. With `in_include` it is `_included_placement` instead (:320, the included side of the deepest mapping).\n    - `AppendTarget`.\n  - Computes `intended` with `splice` (:199).\n- Existing value: `planner._Planner.replace` (`planner.py:275`).\n  - For a mapping parent it uses `physical_entry(parent, key)`, so the override entry if one exists, else the included file's entry. The file is `written.origin.file`.\n  - `change_slot` (:622) runs the no-op check with `plain_equal`. Then, if `node.include is not None`, it calls `_change_include` (:711-753):\n    - A YAML include root is refused with "the whole value comes from the include file:… change the included file instead" (exit 3, naming `at` and the target).\n    - A text include has its target file rewritten.\n  - Otherwise it calls `_change_value` (:660), which edits in place in the file holding the value.\n  - A value inside an included YAML file is edited directly in that file today. Remote includes will need a refusal here (exit 3, include string in stderr), probably by checking the include chain of the node's origin file.\n- New keys (step 5 rule):\n  - `target._walk` (:544-567) walks the holder chain (built by `_holders`, :441) upward.\n  - At the first YAML include it places the keys into the `$ref` form (`_into_form`, :570), or returns `KeyPlacement(convert=IncludeSite)` for a scalar-form include.\n  - The planner applies this in `_Planner._place` (`planner.py:319`). Conversion goes through `convert_include` (:498) and `_convert_root` (:566).\n  - `place_new_keys` (`target.py:279`) is the public variant.\n- Read-back:\n  - `writeback/verify.py:verify_patch` (:24-58) calls `load_config(config_file, overrides)` with every loaded file's text plus the patched texts.\n  - A `ConfigLoadError` becomes `WriteRefusedError` "written value cannot be read back" (exit 3). The result is compared with `plain_equal(root_plain(after), request.intended, ordered=True)`.\n  - Includes newly written by this set are read from disk by the loader, so a remote one would be fetched during verify.\n  - `shared._Loading.content` (`shared.py:450-470`) also loads new includes via `load_include` and turns errors into exit 3.\n\n## (5) How `set` VALUE is parsed and how a `file:` string is written\n- `parse_value(text, path)` (`writeback/value.py:~55`) produces `NewScalar` / `NewMapping` / `NewSequence`:\n  - ruamel round-trip `compose`.\n  - Plain scalars are typed with `resolve_plain_scalar`. Quoted scalars are strings; block scalars are refused.\n  - Flow-only collections. Tags and anchors are refused (`_refuse_properties`). All of these are `UsageError`, exit 2.\n- Decision D (`spec/spec/config-set-writeback/research.md:191-192`, requirements 2.7 and 2.8 at `requirements.md:124-126, 175-176, 304`): a `file:` string is written as an ordinary string scalar. `scalar_render.py:14-15` says "Strings starting with `file:` get no special treatment here (2.7)".\n- Its include meaning comes only from:\n  - `compare.new_to_plain` / `_include_text` (`compare.py:118-146`), which turns it into a contentless `IncludeDirective`.\n  - `plain_equal`, which treats an existing identical include as unchanged.\n  - The verify read-back, which must load it as an include.\n- Where a `!literal` write would hook in: the rendering paths `planner._replace_scalar` (:1363), `_replace_value` (:1279) and `collection_render`.\n\n## (6) CLI flags for `set` (`cli/set_command.py`)\n- `_SetArgumentParser._parse_optional` (:68-72): a single-dash argument other than `-h` is treated as positional.\n- Arguments:\n  - `path` (:115) and `value` (:116).\n  - `--reason TEXT` (:117, default None, `store`; the last one wins).\n  - `--in-include` (:126, `store_true`).\n  - `--expand-alias` (:134, `store_true`).\n- `handle_set` (:76-98) does `ConfigPath.parse` (:88), then `parse_value` (:89), both before the theatre is opened. It then calls `set_value(..., cli_context(args.actor, args.reason), in_include=..., expand_alias=...)` (:91).\n- A new `--include` flag would go here, threaded through `set_value` and `SetRequest` the same way `in_include` is.\n\n## (7) Errors and exit codes\n- `config/errors.py` defines `ExitCode`: OK 0, USAGE 2, WRITE_REFUSED 3, LOAD 4, PATH 5.\n- Classes: `UsageError` → 2, `WriteRefusedError` → 3, `ConfigLoadError` → 4, `PathError` / `PathNotFoundError` / `WrongNodeKindError` → 5.\n- Message format is `<file>: <path>: <problem>` (`format_message`).\n- `cli/main.py:run` (:101-126) catches `TroupeError`, writes `str(error)` to stderr and returns `error.exit_code`. Stdout is buffered and written only on success.\n- For "include string as written in stderr", put it in `problem`, as `planner.py:723-731` already does with `f"file:{include.spec}"`.\n\n## (8) What `watch` watches (`config/watch.py`)\n- `Watcher._watch_paths` (:113-114) is `{source.path for source in document.files} | root candidates`, i.e. every file the last successful load read, plus both root names.\n- The baseline is `_loaded_bytes` (:129) merged with the snapshot (`_sync`, :90-105). Paths are polled with `Path.read_bytes` (`_read_bytes`, :138).\n- Remote `SourceFile`s would land in `document.files`, so `_watch_paths` and `_loaded_bytes` need to skip them, unless polling them is intended. The schema file is not in the watch set today.\n\n## (9) Test conventions\n- `tests/conftest.py`:\n  - Autouse fixture removes `TROUPE_ACTOR`.\n  - `make_theatre(config, *, config_name, files, name)` calls `support.write_theatre`, which writes `<tmp>/theatre/.troupe/troupe.yml` plus `files` (paths relative to the theatre dir, str or bytes).\n  - `run_cli(*args, cwd)` runs the real `troupe` console script as a subprocess and returns `CliResult(exit_code, stdout: bytes, stderr: str)`.\n- `tests/support.py`: `write_theatre` (:169), `invoke_troupe` (:196), `step1_members` / `lineage_fields` helpers.\n- `tests/set_support.py`:\n  - `run_config` (:27).\n  - `run_config_in_process` (:32), which calls `cli_main.run` in process so tests can monkeypatch (useful for faking HTTP).\n  - `set_args` / `run_set` (:45, :53), `assert_file_bytes` (:58), `snapshot_tree` (:65, which ignores the history store).\n- `tests/watch_support.py`: `WatchProcess` (:71).\n- `tests/writeback/` holds unit tests on planner, target and verify. They are importable because `pythonpath = ["tests"]`.\n- e2e tests typically assert `(exit_code, stdout, stderr)` and a one-line stderr `<file>: <path>: …` (e.g. `test_e2e_includes.py:27-39`).\n\n**Tests that pin "explicit tags → exit 4" (all will conflict with `!literal`)**\n- `tests/test_e2e_locate_load.py:210-235`, `test_explicit_tag_exits_4_naming_the_file_and_key_path`. Uses `!!str`, `!custom`, `!!int`; no `!literal` case.\n- `tests/test_loader.py:455-466`, the `REFUSALS` "tag-*" entries such as `! 5`, `!custom x`, `!!str`. Run by the test at :478.\n- `tests/test_loader_aliases.py:316-347`, tags next to anchors.\n- VALUE tags → exit 2: `tests/writeback/test_value.py:140-146` (`"!t x"`, `"! x"`, …) and `tests/test_set_cli.py:96`. These stay valid as long as `!literal` is only written, not accepted in VALUE.\n\n**Tests that pin "http strings are plain strings"**: none. `grep -rn http tests/` finds nothing.\n\n**Existing tests that set `file:` VALUEs (decision D)**\n- `tests/test_set_targets.py:202-231` (new include written as a string; missing target and cycle refused).\n- `tests/test_set_history.py:200,220`.\n- `tests/test_set_overrides.py:918,1398`.\n- `tests/test_e2e_reconcile.py:160`.

tool: Write {"content": "# Brief: config-remote-schema\n\n## Problem\nTeams want one shared configuration for every theatre (step 7). They publish it at an `https://` URL and include\nit from each theatre's `troupe.yml`. troupe must read that shared configuration, but it must never pretend it can\nchange it. A `set` that would have to change a value that comes from the URL has to be refused, and the message\nhas to tell the user which include line in their own file leads there.\n\nOnce `https://` strings are includes, some settings become ambiguous. The schema also\nlets troupe reject a configuration, or a `set`, that does not match the theatre's declared shape.\n\n### Current State\n- config-foundation (`config/loader.py`): only `file:` strings are includes (`INCLUDE_PREFIX`, `_Builder._build`,\n  `_ref_value`). Six places rebuild include strings as `\"file:\" + spec` (`compare.py`,\n  `shared.py`, `planner.py`). Read-back verification reloads every file (`verify.py`).\n- config-include-overrides: new keys under an include are written as overrides in the including file. Until now only `file:` strings\nwere includes, every explicit tag was refused (config-foundation 3.7, decision I), and nothing described the shape\nof a configuration. Step 7 adds:\n- **Remote includes**: `https://` and `http://` strings are includes, fetched each time the configuration loads.\n  Every command reads their content. A failed fetch is a load failure (exit 4).\n- **Read-only remote content**: a `set` that would change anything written in remote content is refused (exit 3),\n  and stderr quotes the include line of the user's own file. New keys below a remote include become local overrides\n  (step 5).\n- **Literals**: a string tagged `!literal`, or whose schema has `\"format\": \"uri\"`, is a plain string, never an include.\n- **A schema**: `.troupe/troupe.schema.json` (JSON Schema 2020-12), when present, must accept the loaded configuration\n  (else exit 4) and the configuration a `set` would produce (else exit 3).\n- **Writing include-like VALUEs**: a VALUE string that would be read as an include is written tagged `!literal`,\n  unless `set --include` is given or the schema marks its path `format: uri`.\n\nTerms used below:\n- **Include string**: a string scalar value (in any quoting style, never a mapping key) that starts with `file:`,\n  `https://` or `http://` (case-sensitive). A **remote include** is one starting with `https://` or `http://`; its\n  **URL** is the whole string.\n- **Remote content**: the content fetched for a remote include, and everything written in it (at any depth, including\n  through further remote includes in it).\n- **Literal**: an include string that is read as a string, not as an include (Requirement 4).\n- **Include-like string**: a string that would be read as an include if written plain at its path: an include string\n  for which...[truncated 22273 chars]

The brief's fourteen open choices are taken as written except\nhistory's schema check: every command loads the configuration today, so history\nfetches and validates too. Eight further choices found while writing are listed\nwith them for the design to record.\nEOF\npython3 - <<'PY'\nimport json\np='spec/spec/config-remote-schema/spec.json'; d=json.load(open(p))\nd['approvals']['requirements']['approved']=True; d['updated_at']='2026-10-07T09:25:00Z'\nopen(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d,indent=2)+\"\\n\")\nPY\ngit add -A spec/spec/config-remote-schema && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nspec(config-remote-schema): record fast-track approval of the requirements\n\nThe product owner is not available for step 7, so the requirements are\napproved under -y with the brief's open-choice defaults as the decisions. The\napproval note in requirements.md says which choice differs from the brief and\nwhy, so a later review knows which decisions are ours to revisit.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -3", "description": "Commit requirements and approval"}

tool: Write {"content": "# Design Document: config-remote-schema\n\n## Overview\nStep 7 lets a theatre include a shared configuration from an `https://` or `http://` URL and describe its\nconfiguration with a JSON Schema. The loader fetches remote includes every time the configuration loads, and every\nreader sees their content. Strings tagged `!literal`, or whose schema says `\"format\": \"uri\"`, stay plain strings.\nThe loaded configuration must validate against `.troupe/troupe.schema.json` when that file exists. `troupe config\nset` refuses to change anything written in remote content and quotes the local include line. It refuses a result\nthat does not validate, and writes include-like VALUE strings tagged `!literal` unless `--include` is given.\n\nThe loader decides what an include is, in one place, while it walks the tree with the current path. A remote\nsource is an in-memory `SourceFile` with a URL identity, so layers, overrides, `--sources`, leaves, reconcile and\nhistory work unchanged. Only the writer needs to know that such a source is read-only. Schema validation happens in\nthe loader, so the read-back of a `set` (config-set-writeback 13.6) also checks the intended configuration against\nthe schema before anything is written.\n\n### Goals\n- Remote includes load, fail with exit 4, and appear in every reader (Req 1, 2, 3).\n- Literals from `!literal` and `format: uri` (Req 4). The schema is read and enforced on load (Req 5) and on `set`\n  (Req 7).\n- Remote content is read-only and the refusal names the local include line (Req 6).\n- Include-like VALUE strings are written `!literal` unless `--include` is given (Req 8, 9). All earlier guarantees\n  still hold (Req 10).\n\n### Non-Goals\n- Authentication, custom headers, caching, offline use, remote schemas, tags other than `!literal`, format\n  assertion, `file:` includes in remote content.\n- Any change to rendering rules, events, lineage, reconcile, override placement or alias expansion beyond the points\n  name...[truncated 33587 chars]

',line)\n    if m and cur: ids.append(f\"{cur}.{m.group(1)}\")\nprint(len(ids))\nprint([i for i in ids if f\"| {i} |\" not in des])\nPY", "description": "Check design length and traceability coverage"}

tool: Write {"content": "# Research & Design Decisions: config-remote-schema\n\n## Summary\n- **Feature**: `config-remote-schema` (step 7, contract section \"Remote includes and schema\")\n- **Discovery Scope**: Extension (light discovery: loader, model and writeback integration points, plus checks of\n  ruamel.yaml's handling of tags, the `jsonschema` library and `urllib`)\n- **Key Findings**:\n  - Include recognition is two prefix tests in the loader (`_Builder._build`, `_ref_value`), and the builder already\n    knows the full configuration path of every node it builds, including inside included files (`builder.build(\n    composed.root, path)`). So the literal decision (`!literal`, schema `format: uri`) can be made right there,\n    before anything is read or fetched.\n  - Every edit the planner makes to a file goes through `_Planner._layout(file)` (four call sites, all for editing)\n    or `_Planner._replace_file` (text-include rewrites). These two places are an exact choke point for \"never write\n    remote content\". The alias expander builds its own layout (`expand.py`), and `write_atomically` is the final\n    backstop.\n  - The read-back (`verify_patch`) and the alias expansion loop reload through `load_config(config_file,\n    overrides)`. If overrides are keyed by source identity, remote text is reused and nothing is fetched twice. If\n    the loader validates the schema, the read-back checks the intended configuration against the schema for free.\n\n## Research Log\n\n### Loader, model and readers\n- **Context**: where remote sources and literals enter, and who assumes a local `Path`.\n- **Sources Consulted**: `config/loader.py`, `model.py`, `compose.py`, `resolve.py`, `theatre.py`, `watch.py`,\n  `cli/render.py`, `cli/main.py`.\n- **Findings**:\n  - `SourceFile.real_path` is the identity for the read cache, cycles, overrides, written locations\n    (`shared.written_location`), planner layouts and setter texts (39 uses outside the loader). `SourceFile.path`\n    i...[truncated 18869 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'PY'\nimport json\np='spec/spec/config-remote-schema/spec.json'; d=json.load(open(p))\nd['phase']='design-generated'; d['approvals']['design']['generated']=True; d['approvals']['design']['approved']=False; d['updated_at']='2026-10-07T10:10:00Z'\nopen(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d,indent=2)+\"\\n\")\nPY\ngit add -A spec/spec/config-remote-schema && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nspec(config-remote-schema): design include decisions while walking, URL-identified sources and a layout write guard\n\nThe loader already knows each node's configuration path, so the literal\ndecision (!literal, schema format: uri) is made before anything is fetched.\nRemote content becomes ordinary SourceFiles with a URL identity, so every\nreader works unchanged. Every planner edit passes one layout choke point,\nwhich is where writes into remote content are refused. Schema validation\nlives in the loader, so a set's read-back also checks the schema before any\nwrite. research.md records the jsonschema, urllib and ruamel findings and\nthe 22 choices with what would make us revisit them.\nEOF\npython3 - <<'PY'\nimport json\np='spec/spec/config-remote-schema/spec.json'; d=json.load(open(p))\nd['approvals']['design']['approved']=True; d['updated_at']='2026-10-07T10:15:00Z'\nopen(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d,indent=2)+\"\\n\")\nPY\ngit add -A spec/spec/config-remote-schema && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nspec(config-remote-schema): record fast-track approval of the design\n\nThe product owner is not available, so the design is approved under -y.\nThe out-of-contract choices it relies on (SourceFile identity by key/shown,\nthe schema check done at read-back, the layout choke point) are recorded in\nresearch.md as our decisions, so they can be revisited.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -2", "description": "Commit design and its approval"}
tool: Write {"content": "# Implementation Plan\n\n**Conventions.** Every test written for this spec names the requirement IDs it proves in its docstring (for example\n`\"\"\"Req 6.1: ...\"\"\"`), as in the earlier specs. Tests use real theatres on disk, the real CLI and real HTTP from a\nlocal server, never the internet (`testing.md`). Preservation tests compare whole files byte for byte, and error\ntests assert the file, the key path and, for remote cases, the URL. Each task ends with `uv run pytest -n auto`,\n`uv run mypy --strict .`, `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .` passing. Existing tests that pin\nbehaviour a task changes are updated in that same task.\n\n**Where this spec's tests live.** Every test written for this spec goes in one of these new files. Existing test files\nonly lose, adjust or gain rows that pin a changed rule. This keeps the traceability check of task 5.1 exact.\n- `tests/remote_support.py` (fixture, 1.3), `tests/test_includes.py` (1.1, 1.2), `tests/test_fetch.py` (1.3),\n  `tests/test_schema.py` (1.4)\n- `tests/test_loader_literals.py` (2.1), `tests/test_loader_schema.py` (2.2), `tests/test_loader_remote.py` (2.3, 2.4)\n- `tests/writeback/test_value_marks.py` (3.1, 3.2), `tests/writeback/test_remote_guard.py` (3.3, 3.4)\n- `tests/test_e2e_remote.py` (4.1), `tests/test_set_remote.py` (2.3, 4.2), `tests/test_set_schema.py` (4.3),\n  `tests/test_set_literals.py` (3.5, 4.4)\n\n**External prerequisites.** config-foundation, config-set-writeback, config-file-sync, config-get-show,\nconfig-lineage, config-include-overrides and config-aliases are complete. Task 1.1 adds the only new dependencies:\n`jsonschema` (runtime) and `types-jsonschema` (dev).\n\n**Safety window.** From task 2.3 the loader reads remote sources, but the writer refuses writes into them only from\ntask 3.3. In between, `set` refuses (exit 3, no file changed) every configuration that holds a remote include, and\n`write_atomically` refuses any remote source. Task 2.3 add...[truncated 18190 chars]

',line)\n    if m and cur: ids.append(f\"{cur}.{m.group(1)}\")\nrefs=set()\nfor m in re.finditer(r'_Requirements: ([^_]+)_',t):\n    refs|={x.strip() for x in m.group(1).split(',')}\nprint('missing',[i for i in ids if i not in refs]); print('unknown',sorted(refs-set(ids)))\nprint('subtasks',len(re.findall(r'^- \\[ \\] \\d+\\.\\d+',t,re.M)))\nPY\nwc -l tasks.md", "description": "Fix test-file mapping and check task coverage"}

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/src/troupe/config/constants.py", "new_string": "REMOTE_INCLUDE_PREFIXES: Final = (\"https://\", \"http://\")\n\"\"\"Prefixes (case-sensitive) of a string value that includes remote content; the whole string is its URL.\n\nA remote target is embedded as parsed YAML when its URL path, without query and fragment, ends in one of\n:data:`YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES`, and as its exact text otherwise.\n\"\"\"\n\nLITERAL_TAG: Final = \"!literal\"\n\"\"\"The one explicit YAML tag the loader accepts: a scalar so tagged is a plain string, never an include.\"\"\"\n\nSCHEMA_FILE_NAME: Final = \"troupe.schema.json\"\n\"\"\"File name of the theatre's optional JSON Schema, next to the root configuration file.\"\"\"\n\nSCHEMA_DIALECT: Final = \"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\"\n\"\"\"The only JSON Schema dialect accepted as the schema's ``$schema``.\"\"\"\n\nREMOTE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS: Final = 10\n\"\"\"Longest wait in seconds for a server answering a remote include fetch.\"\"\"\n\nHISTORY_STORE_NAME: Final = \"history.sqlite\"", "old_string": "HISTORY_STORE_NAME: Final = \"history.sqlite\"", "replace_all": false}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Include recognition: the one place that says whether a string would be an include, and of what kind.\n\nA string value starting with :data:`~troupe.config.constants.INCLUDE_PREFIX` or one of\n:data:`~troupe.config.constants.REMOTE_INCLUDE_PREFIXES` (case-sensitive) is an include string. Whether\nsuch a string actually is an include is the loader's decision (a ``!literal`` tag or a schema\n``format: uri`` makes it a plain string); these functions only say it would be one if written plain.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import PurePosixPath\nfrom urllib.parse import urlsplit\n\nfrom troupe.config.constants import INCLUDE_PREFIX, REMOTE_INCLUDE_PREFIXES, YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES\nfrom troupe.config.scalars import ScalarValue\n\n_INCLUDE_PREFIXES = (INCLUDE_PREFIX, *REMOTE_INCLUDE_PREFIXES)\n\n\ndef is_include_string(value: ScalarValue) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Whether ``value`` is a string starting with ``file:``, ``https://`` or ``http://``.\"\"\"\n    return isinstance(value, str) and value.startswith(_INCLUDE_PREFIXES)\n\n\ndef is_remote_include(text: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Whether the include string ``text`` is remote: it starts with ``https://`` or ``http://``.\"\"\"\n    return text.startswith(REMOTE_INCLUDE_PREFIXES)\n\n\ndef include_is_yaml(text: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Whether the include string ``text`` targets YAML content rather than text.\n\n    A remote include is YAML when its URL path, without query and fragment, ends in a YAML suffix. A\n    ``file:`` include is YAML when its target's name does, as before step 7. Suffixes are case-sensitive.\n    \"\"\"\n    if is_remote_include(text):\n        return urlsplit(text).path.endswith(YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES)\n    return PurePosixPath(text.removeprefix(INCLUDE_PREFIX)).name.endswith(YAML_INCLUDE_SUFFIXES)\n", "file_path": "/work/repo/src/troupe/config/includes.py"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The configuration's JSON Schema: reading it, the ``format: uri`` walker and validation.\n\nThis module is the only JSON Schema code (config-remote-schema Requirements 4.4, 4.5, 5.1-5.5).\n\n* :func:`read_schema` reads ``<config dir>/troupe.schema.json``. An absent file means no schema;\n  a file that cannot be used is a :class:`ConfigLoadError` naming it.\n* :func:`uri_format_at` says whether the schema gives a path's subschema ``\"format\": \"uri\"``. It\n  walks ``properties``, ``items`` (digit-only segments) and ``additionalProperties`` only, each\n  only when it is an object subschema; ``$ref``, combinators, ``patternProperties`` and\n  ``prefixItems`` are never followed.\n* :func:`validate` checks the expanded configuration with the 2020-12 validator. ``format`` is an\n  annotation (no format checker). One failure, the best match, is reported. No ``$ref`` is ever\n  retrieved: an unresolvable one, local or remote, is a :class:`ConfigLoadError` naming the schema\n  file.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom jsonschema.exceptions import SchemaError, best_match\nfrom referencing.exceptions import Unresolvable\n\nfrom troupe.config.constants import PATH_SEPARATOR, SCHEMA_DIALECT, SCHEMA_FILE_NAME\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError, SchemaViolationError\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping, ConfigNode, ConfigScalar, JsonValue, SchemaSource\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath, is_index_segment\n\n_URI_FORMAT = \"uri\"\n\n\ndef read_schema(config_dir: Path) -> SchemaSource | None:\n    \"\"\"Read and check ``config_dir/troupe.schema.json``; ``None`` when the file does not exist.\n\n    Raises :class:`ConfigLoadError` naming the schema file when it is not a regular file, cannot\n    be read, is not UTF-8 or JSON, is not an object or boolean, has a ``$schema`` other than the\n    2020-12 dialect, or is not a valid 2020-12 schema.\n    \"\"\"\n ...[truncated 4523 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-remote-schema/research.md'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=\"\"\"  - A `$ref` to `https://…` is **not** retrieved by default. Validation raises\n    `jsonschema.exceptions._WrappedReferencingError`, which is a `referencing.exceptions.Unresolvable`. A missing\n    local `#/$defs/x` raises the same. Both are raised lazily, when validation meets the `$ref`.\"\"\"\nnew=\"\"\"  - A `$ref` to an unresolvable host raises `jsonschema.exceptions._WrappedReferencingError`, which is a\n    `referencing.exceptions.Unresolvable`. A missing local `#/$defs/x` raises the same. Both are raised lazily,\n    when validation meets the `$ref`.\n  - *Corrected during task 1.3*: without an explicit registry, jsonschema 4.26 falls back to its deprecated\n    resolver and **does** fetch a reachable `https://…` `$ref` over HTTP (only a DeprecationWarning is issued;\n    proven against a local HTTP server). The validator is therefore built with an empty\n    `referencing.Registry()`, so nothing is retrieved and a remote `$ref` is unresolvable (exit 4, Req 5.3).\n    Every validation must go through `schema.validate()` to keep that registry.\"\"\"\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='spec/spec/config-remote-schema/tasks.md'\ns=open(p).read().replace(\"- [ ] 1.3 \",\"- [x] 1.3 \",1)\ns=s.rstrip(\"\\n\")+\"\\n\\n## Implementation Notes\\n- 1.3: jsonschema 4.26 fetches remote `$ref`s unless a registry is passed; always validate through `schema.validate()` (empty `referencing.Registry()`).\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/schema.py tests/test_schema.py spec/spec/config-remote-schema/tasks.md spec/spec/config-remote-schema/research.md; git commit -q -m \"feat(config-remote-schema): read the schema, find format: uri and validate a configuration\n\nThe literal decision and the validity checks of loads and sets all need the same schema reading\nand the same properties/items/additionalPropert...[truncated 221 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"A real local HTTP server for remote include tests (config-remote-schema).\n\n:class:`RemoteServer` runs a ``ThreadingHTTPServer`` on ``127.0.0.1`` with an ephemeral port in a\ndaemon thread. Tests change its route table while it runs, and it records every request it\nreceives. It serves in-process unit tests and the real ``troupe`` console script alike, since the\nsubprocess reaches the same port. No test ever reaches the internet.\n\nThe ``remote_server`` fixture in ``tests/conftest.py`` starts one per test, sets ``NO_PROXY`` (the\nCLI subprocess inherits it) and stops the server afterwards.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport threading\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer\nfrom types import TracebackType\nfrom typing import Self\n\nNO_PROXY_HOSTS = \"127.0.0.1,localhost\"\n\"\"\"Hosts that must bypass any proxy configured in the developer's shell.\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Route:\n    \"\"\"What the server answers for one path: a status, body bytes and extra headers.\n\n    ``delay`` seconds pass before the answer starts (the timeout tests). ``Content-Length`` is\n    always sent for ``body``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status: int = 200\n    body: bytes = b\"\"\n    headers: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    delay: float = 0.0\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RecordedRequest:\n    \"\"\"One request the server received: method, path (with query) and headers.\"\"\"\n\n    method: str\n    path: str\n    headers: dict[str, str]\n\n\nclass RemoteServer:\n    \"\"\"A threaded HTTP server on 127.0.0.1 with a mutable route table and a request log.\n\n    Unknown paths answer ``404 Not Found``. Use it as a context manager, or call :meth:`start`\n    and :meth:`stop`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self.routes: dict[str, Route] = {}\n        self.requests: list[RecordedRequest] = []\n        self._lock = threading.Lock()\n        self._stopping = ...[truncated 4135 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/conftest.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('from pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport pytest\\n\\nfrom support import','from collections.abc import Iterator\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport pytest\\n\\nfrom remote_support import NO_PROXY_HOSTS, RemoteServer\\nfrom support import')\ns+='''\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef remote_server(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> Iterator[RemoteServer]:\n    \"\"\"Fixture: a real HTTP server on 127.0.0.1 (see ``RemoteServer``), stopped afterwards.\n\n    ``NO_PROXY``/``no_proxy`` are set so that neither in-process fetches nor the CLI subprocess\n    (which inherits the environment) go through a proxy from the developer's shell.\n    \"\"\"\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"NO_PROXY\", NO_PROXY_HOSTS)\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"no_proxy\", NO_PROXY_HOSTS)\n    with RemoteServer() as server:\n        yield server\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Fetching remote sources over real HTTP (config-remote-schema).\n\nEvery test runs against the local ``remote_server`` fixture; nothing reaches the internet and the\nfetcher is never mocked.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport time\nimport urllib.request\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom remote_support import NO_PROXY_HOSTS, RemoteServer\nfrom troupe.config.constants import REMOTE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS\nfrom troupe.config.fetch import FetchError, fetch_remote\n\n\ndef _reason(url: str, *, timeout: float = REMOTE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) -> str:\n    with pytest.raises(FetchError) as caught:\n        fetch_remote(url, timeout=timeout)\n    assert str(caught.value) == caught.value.reason\n    return caught.value.reason\n\n\ndef test_fetch_returns_the_exact_body_bytes_of_a_200(remote_server: RemoteServer) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 1.6: a 200 answer gives its body bytes unchanged (decoding is the loader's job).\"\"\"\n    body = b\"a: 1\\r\\nb: caf\\xc3\\xa9\\n\\xff\"\n    url = remote_server.route(\"/shared.yml\", body, headers={\"Content-Type\": \"text/yaml\"})\n\n    assert fetch_remote(url) == body\n\n\ndef test_fetch_accepts_any_2xx_status(remote_server: RemoteServer) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.1: only a final status outside 2xx fails; 203 is a success.\"\"\"\n    url = remote_server.route(\"/x.txt\", b\"text\", status=203)\n\n    assert fetch_remote(url) == b\"text\"\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"status\", \"reason\"),\n    [\n        pytest.param(404, \"HTTP 404 Not Found\", id=\"404\"),\n        pytest.param(500, \"HTTP 500 Internal Server Error\", id=\"500\"),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_fetch_refuses_a_non_2xx_status_with_its_code_and_phrase(\n    remote_server: RemoteServer, status: int, reason: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 2.1: a final status outside 2xx is a fetch error giving the status as its reason.\"\"\"\n    url = remote_server.rout...[truncated 3948 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Fetching remote sources: the only network code in troupe (config-remote-schema).\n\n:func:`fetch_remote` sends one plain GET with urllib's default headers, follows redirects and\nreturns the body bytes. Every failure is a :class:`FetchError` with a short reason. It sends no\ncredentials and writes nothing to disk (Requirement 10.4). Decoding the bytes and turning a failure\ninto a load error are the loader's job.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport socket\nfrom http.client import HTTPException\nfrom urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError\nfrom urllib.request import Request, urlopen\n\nfrom troupe.config.constants import REMOTE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS\n\n\nclass FetchError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"A remote source could not be fetched; ``reason`` says why in a few words.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, reason: str) -> None:\n        super().__init__(reason)\n        self.reason = reason\n\n\ndef fetch_remote(url: str, *, timeout: float = REMOTE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"GET ``url`` once, following redirects, and return the final 2xx answer's body bytes.\n\n    Raises :class:`FetchError` when the server cannot be reached, does not answer within\n    ``timeout`` seconds, or answers with a final status outside 200-299.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        with urlopen(Request(url, method=\"GET\"), timeout=timeout) as response:\n            status = int(response.status)\n            if not 200 <= status <= 299:\n                raise FetchError(f\"HTTP {status} {response.reason}\")\n            body: bytes = response.read()\n            return body\n    except HTTPError as error:\n        raise FetchError(f\"HTTP {error.code} {error.reason}\") from error\n    except URLError as error:\n        raise FetchError(_os_reason(error.reason)) from error\n    except (TimeoutError, OSError) as error:\n        raise FetchError(_os_reason(error)) from error\n    except HTTPException as error:\n        raise FetchError(f\"bad HTTP answer ({type(error).__name__})\") from err...[truncated 661 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_fetch.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('import os\\nimport time\\n','import os\\nimport subprocess\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\n')\ns+='''\n\ndef test_remote_server_answers_a_separate_process(remote_server: RemoteServer) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 1.6: a subprocess (as the CLI e2e tests run) fetches from the fixture and is logged.\"\"\"\n    url = remote_server.route(\"/shared.yml\", b\"from: server\\\\n\")\n    script = \"import sys, troupe.config.fetch as f; sys.stdout.buffer.write(f.fetch_remote(sys.argv[1]))\"\n\n    completed = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, \"-c\", script, url], capture_output=True, check=False, timeout=30\n    )\n\n    assert completed.returncode == 0, completed.stderr\n    assert completed.stdout == b\"from: server\\\\n\"\n    assert remote_server.paths() == [\"/shared.yml\"]\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_fetch.py tests/test_schema.py 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run mypy --strict 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check ."}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Loading ``!literal`` scalars (config-remote-schema 4.1-4.3, 4.6; task 2.1).\n\nEvery test writes a real theatre and loads it with ``load_config`` (or runs the real CLI). A scalar\ntagged exactly ``!literal`` loads as its text, in any style and without plain-scalar typing, records\nthe tag's span in ``ConfigScalar.literal``, and its own span excludes the tag, as for an anchor\n(``&a !literal x`` loads). It is never an include. ``!literal`` on a mapping, a list or a mapping\nkey, ``!literal`` written before an anchor, a ``$ref`` value tagged ``!literal`` and every other tag\nstay load errors (exit 4) naming the file, the key path and the tag.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport hashlib\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre, RunCli\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError, ExitCode\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigScalar,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    ScalarStyle,\n    Span,\n)\n\nCONFIG_NAMES = [\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"]\n\n\ndef _config(theatre: Path, name: str = \"troupe.yml\") -> Path:\n    return theatre / \".troupe\" / name\n\n\ndef _load(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, text: str, files: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> Path:\n    return _config(make_theatre(text, files=files))\n\n\ndef _node_at(document: ConfigDocument, *keys: str | int) -> ConfigNode:\n    node: ConfigNode = document.root\n    for key in keys:\n        if isinstance(key, int):\n            assert isinstance(node, ConfigSequence)\n            node = node.items[key]\n        else:\n            assert isinstance(node, ConfigMapping)\n            entry = node.get(key)\n            assert entry is not None, key\n            node = entry.value\n    return node\n\n\ndef _scalar_at(document: ConfigDocument, *keys: str | int) -> ConfigScalar:\n    ...[truncated 15043 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /tmp && rm -rf lt && mkdir -p lt/.troupe && cd lt && cat > /tmp/probe.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError\ncases = [\n 'a: !literal \"a\\\\nb\"\\n',\n 'a: !literal |\\n  x\\n  y\\nb: 1\\n',\n 'a: !literal |-\\n  file:x.yml\\n',\n 'a: !literal >-\\n  one\\n  two\\n',\n 'a: !literal multi\\n  line plain\\n',\n 'a: !literal\\tx\\n',\n 'l: [!literal , 2]\\n',\n 'l: [!literal, 2]\\n',\n 'f: {k: !literal}\\n',\n 'f: {k: !literal , j: 1}\\n',\n 'a: &x !literal\\n',\n 'a:\\n  b: !literal \"\"\\n',\n '- !literal x\\n',\n 'a: !literal\\n  - 1\\n',\n 'a: ! x\\n',\n 'a: !literal 0o17\\n',\n 'a: !literal .inf\\n',\n 'a:\\n  $ref: file:inc.yml\\n  k: !literal file:z\\n',\n 'a: !literal &x\\n',\n]\np = Path('.troupe/troupe.yml'); Path('.troupe/inc.yml').write_text('k: v\\n')\nfor c in cases:\n    p.write_text(c)\n    try:\n        d = load_config(p)\n        r = d.root\n        out=[]\n        def walk(n, pre=''):\n            from troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping, ConfigSequence\n            if isinstance(n, ConfigMapping):\n                for e in n.entries: walk(e.value, pre+'.'+str(e.key))\n            elif isinstance(n, ConfigSequence):\n                for i,x in enumerate(n.items): walk(x, pre+f'.{i}')\n            else:\n                out.append((pre, repr(n.value), n.literal and c[n.literal.start:n.literal.end], repr(c[n.origin.span.start:n.origin.span.end]), n.include is not None))\n        walk(r)\n        print(repr(c), '->', out)\n    except ConfigLoadError as e:\n        print(repr(c), 'ERR', e.file.name if e.file else None, e.path, e.problem)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(repr(c), 'EXC', type(e).__name__, e)\nEOF\ncd /tmp/lt && uv run --project /work/repo python /tmp/probe.py"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Reading and enforcing the schema when the configuration loads (config-remote-schema; task 2.2).\n\nEvery test writes a real theatre with a real ``troupe.schema.json`` next to the root configuration\nfile and loads it with ``load_config`` (or runs the real CLI). A string whose own path the schema\ngives ``\"format\": \"uri\"`` stays a string, in the root file and in included files (4.4, 4.6). The\nexpanded configuration (includes, overrides, aliases, merge keys and text includes as their\nvalues) is validated; a configuration that does not match, or a schema that cannot be used, is a\nload error (exit 4) naming the schema file, with no history state created and the schema file\nuntouched (5.1-5.4, 5.6). Without a schema file, loading is unchanged (5.4).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport hashlib\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre, RunCli\nfrom troupe.config.constants import SCHEMA_FILE_NAME\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError, ExitCode, SchemaViolationError\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config, load_include\nfrom troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigScalar,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    IncludeKind,\n    JsonValue,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.schema import read_schema\n\nCONFIG_NAMES = [\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"]\n\nURI: dict[str, JsonValue] = {\"type\": \"string\", \"format\": \"uri\"}\n\n\ndef _theatre(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n    config: str,\n    schema: JsonValue | str | None,\n    files: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n    config_name: str = \"troupe.yml\",\n) -> Path:\n    written = dict(files or {})\n    if schema is not None:\n        text = schema if isinstance(schema, str) else json.dumps(schema)\n        written[f\".troupe/{SCHEMA_FILE_NAME}\"] = text\n    return make_theatr...[truncated 19761 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport re\np='tests/test_loader_schema.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''def _violation(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, schema: JsonValue, **files: str) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Load a configuration the schema rejects; return the schema path after checking the error.\"\"\"\n    theatre = _theatre(make_theatre, config, schema, {f\".troupe/{k}\": v for k, v in files.items()})\n    with pytest.raises(SchemaViolationError) as caught:\n        load_config(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    assert caught.value.exit_code == ExitCode.LOAD\n    assert caught.value.file == theatre / \".troupe\" / SCHEMA_FILE_NAME\n    _last_violation.clear()\n    _last_violation.append(caught.value)\n    return theatre / \".troupe\" / SCHEMA_FILE_NAME\n\n\n_last_violation: list[SchemaViolationError] = []\n''','''def _violation(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, schema: JsonValue, **files: str\n) -> SchemaViolationError:\n    \"\"\"Load a configuration the schema rejects; return the error after checking that it is a load\n    error (exit 4) naming the schema file.\"\"\"\n    theatre = _theatre(make_theatre, config, schema, {f\".troupe/{k}\": v for k, v in files.items()})\n    with pytest.raises(SchemaViolationError) as caught:\n        load_config(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    assert caught.value.exit_code == ExitCode.LOAD\n    assert caught.value.file == theatre / \".troupe\" / SCHEMA_FILE_NAME\n    return caught.value\n''')\n# replace patterns: \"    _violation(\" first statement lines followed by _last_violation[0]\ns=re.sub(r'\\n(    )(_violation\\()', r'\\n\\1error = \\2', s)\ns=s.replace('    error = _last_violation[0]\\n','')\ns=s.replace('_last_violation[0]','error')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"error = _violation\\|_last\" tests/test_loader_schema.py"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Loading remote includes (config-remote-schema; task 2.4).\n\nEvery test serves real HTTP from the local ``remote_server`` fixture and loads a real theatre\nwith ``load_config`` (or runs the real CLI); nothing reaches the internet and the fetcher is never\nmocked. A remote include (``https://`` or ``http://``, in the scalar form or as the ``$ref`` of a\nmapping-form include) is fetched once per load, decoded strictly as UTF-8 and read as YAML or text\nby its URL path's suffix; it becomes a source with that URL (1.1, 1.2, 1.6). Remote YAML may hold\nremote includes, not ``file:`` ones, and a URL cycle is refused (1.3-1.5). Every failure is a load\nerror naming the including file by its display name, the key path and the URL (2.1, 2.2), and the\nCLI then exits 4 changing nothing (2.3).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport socket\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom remote_support import RemoteServer\nfrom set_support import snapshot_tree\nfrom support import MakeTheatre, RunCli\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError, ExitCode\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config, load_include\nfrom troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigScalar,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    IncludeKind,\n)\n\nCONFIG_NAMES = [\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"]\n\n\ndef _load(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, files: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> ConfigDocument:\n    theatre = make_theatre(config, files=files)\n    return load_config(theatre / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n\n\ndef _refusal(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, config: str, files: dict[str, str] | None = None\n) -> ConfigLoadError:\n    with pytest.raises(ConfigLoadError) as caught:\n        _load(make_theatre, config, files)\n    return caught.value\n\n\ndef _plain(node: ConfigNode) -> object:\n    if isinstance(node, ConfigMapping):\n        return {entr...[truncated 18002 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Sets on configurations with remote content (config-remote-schema).\n\nTask 2.4 pins the temporary refusal that keeps every write away from remote content until the\nremote write guard exists (task 3.3): ``set`` refuses (exit 3, no file changed) any configuration\nthat has a remote source, and ``write_atomically`` never writes a remote source. Every CLI test\nruns the real console script against the local ``remote_server`` fixture.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom remote_support import RemoteServer\nfrom set_support import run_set, snapshot_tree\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ExitCode, WriteRefusedError\nfrom troupe.config.model import LineEnding, SourceFile\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.atomic import write_atomically\n\nCONFIG_NAMES = [\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"config_name\", CONFIG_NAMES)\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"path\", \"value\"),\n    [\n        pytest.param(\"local\", \"2\", id=\"local-value\"),\n        pytest.param(\"s.a\", \"9\", id=\"remote-value\"),\n        pytest.param(\"s.new\", \"1\", id=\"new-key-below-remote\"),\n        pytest.param(\"local\", \"1\", id=\"no-op\"),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_set_refuses_every_configuration_with_a_remote_source(\n    make_theatre: object,\n    remote_server: RemoteServer,\n    config_name: str,\n    path: str,\n    value: str,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 6.1, 10.1: until the remote write guard exists, a set on a configuration holding a\n    remote include exits 3 with nothing on stdout, stderr naming the root file, PATH and the URL,\n    and no user file changed.\"\"\"\n    url = remote_server.route(\"/s.yml\", \"a: 1\\n\")\n    assert callable(make_theatre)\n    theatre: Path = make_theatre(f\"local: 1\\ns: {url}\\n\", config_name=config_name)\n    before = snapshot_tree(theatre)\n\n    result = run_set(theatre, path, value)\n\n    ...[truncated 1190 chars]

tool: Edit {"file_path": "/work/repo/src/troupe/config/loader.py", "new_string": "included several times (a diamond) is shared and listed once in ``ConfigDocument.files``.\n\nRemote includes (config-remote-schema 1.1-1.6, 2.1, 2.2): an include string starting with one of\n:data:`REMOTE_INCLUDE_PREFIXES` (recognised by :mod:`troupe.config.includes`) is a URL. It is\nfetched (:mod:`troupe.config.fetch`) at most once per load, or taken from the overrides under the\nURL, decoded strictly as UTF-8 and read as YAML or text by\n:func:`~troupe.config.includes.include_is_yaml`. It becomes a :class:`SourceFile` with ``url`` set,\nlisted with the files; cycles and caches compare :attr:`SourceFile.key`. Remote YAML may hold remote\nincludes but no ``file:`` include. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/t32_probe.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys\nsys.path.insert(0, \"tests\")\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport tempfile\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import *  # noqa\nfrom troupe.config.writeback import value as V\nimport troupe.config.writeback.value as vm\nprint([n for n in dir(vm) if not n.startswith(\"_\")])\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/t32_probe.py; grep -n \"^from\\|^def _mark\\|mark_include_strings\\|parse_value\" tests/writeback/test_value_marks.py | head; grep -n \"make_theatre\" -A25 tests/support.py | head -40"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/t32_probe.py <<'EOF'\nimport tempfile, traceback\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_patch\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import mark_include_strings, parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import node_to_plain, plain_equal\n\ndef run(config, path, value):\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); (d/\".troupe\").mkdir(); f = d/\".troupe\"/\"troupe.yml\"; f.write_text(config)\n    try:\n        doc = load_config(f); p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n        m = mark_include_strings(parse_value(value, p), p, doc.schema, include=False)\n        req = resolve_target(doc, p, m)\n        patches = plan_patch(doc, req)\n        texts = {x.file.key: x.new_text for x in patches}\n        out = [x.new_text for x in patches]\n        r = load_config(f, texts)\n        ok = plain_equal(node_to_plain(r.root), req.intended, ordered=True)\n        print(repr(config), path, value, \"->\", out, \"readback ok\" if ok else \"READBACK MISMATCH\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(repr(config), path, value, \"-> EXC\", type(e).__name__, e)\n\nrun(\"a: !literal &x https://old\\nb: *x\\n\", \"a\", \"5\")\nrun(\"a: !literal &x https://old\\n\", \"a\", \"5\")\nrun(\"a: !literal &x https://old\\n\", \"a\", \"https://n\")\nrun(\"l:\\n- !literal &x file:old\\n\", \"l.0\", \"2\")\nrun(\"a: !literal &x |\\n  file:x\\nb: 1\\n\", \"a\", \"z\")\nrun(\"a: &x !literal https://old\\nb: *x\\n\", \"a\", \"https://n\")\nrun(\"p: {a: !literal &x https://old, b: 1}\\n\", \"p.a\", \"2\")\nrun(\"a: !literal https://old\\n\", \"a\", \"'https://old'\")\nrun(\"a:\\n  !literal https://old\\n\", \"a\", \"5\")\nrun(\"a: !literal\\n  https://old\\n\", \"a\", \"5\")\nrun(\"a: !<!literal> https://old\\n\", \"a\", \"5\")\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/t32_probe.py"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; git status --porcelain; sed -i 's/^- \\[ \\] 3.2 /- [x] 3.2 /' spec/spec/config-remote-schema/tasks.md\necho '- 3.2: unit tests that reload include-like VALUEs should use hosts that never leave the machine (`.invalid` or the `remote_server` fixture), so a tagging regression fails locally instead of fetching; existing `https://x` fixtures are to be swept before final validation.' >> spec/spec/config-remote-schema/tasks.md\ngit add src/troupe/config/writeback/scalar_render.py src/troupe/config/writeback/collection_render.py src/troupe/config/writeback/layout.py src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py src/troupe/config/writeback/expand.py tests/writeback/test_value_marks.py spec/spec/config-remote-schema/tasks.md; git commit -q -m \"feat(config-remote-schema): write tagged strings and replace existing !literal scalars whole\n\nA literal-marked string only stays a value if its tag is on disk, so the renderer writes\n'!literal ' before it and never as a block scalar, a replaced !literal scalar loses its tag with\nits text, and an alias expansion copies the tag so expanding never turns a literal into an\ninclude.\"; git log --oneline -1"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"RemoteGuard: the refusal of every write into remote content (config-remote-schema 6.1, 6.2).\n\nRemote content is read-only from troupe's point of view. The planner and the alias expander ask\n:func:`remote_refusal` for the error to raise whenever an edit would go into a remote source\n(research.md Decision 5). The error tells the user which include line to change instead: the\nlocal file holding the outermost include directive that leads to the remote source, PATH, the\ndirective's text exactly as written there (quotes included) and its 1-based line.\n\nThe directive is looked up among the include references of the loaded document. The ones met on\nPATH's walk come first, then those at or below PATH's deepest node, then every other one in\ndocument order, so that a file included at several places is reported at PATH's own place. While\nthe directive found is written in a remote source (remote in remote, 6.2), the lookup continues\nwith the include whose target is that source, until the directive lies in a local file.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator\nfrom typing import Final\n\nfrom troupe.config.errors import WriteRefusedError\nfrom troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    IncludeRef,\n    SourceFile,\n)\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.resolve import walk_path\n\nREMOTE_READ_ONLY: Final = (\n    \"the value comes from the remote include {string} (line {line}); troupe does not change \"\n    \"remote content: change that include line or the shared configuration\"\n)\n\"\"\"The problem of a write into remote content; ``{string}`` is the include directive as written\nin the local file and ``{line}`` its 1-based line (6.1).\"\"\"\n\n\ndef remote_refusal(\n    document: ConfigDocument, source: SourceFile, path: ConfigPath\n) -> WriteRefusedError:\n    \"\"\"The refusal (exit 3) of a write into the remote ``source`` at ``path``.\n\n  ...[truncated 3056 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of the remote write guard (config-remote-schema task 3.3):\n``troupe.config.writeback.remote_guard.remote_refusal`` and its use by the PatchPlanner and the\nalias expander.\n\nThe directive lookup is proved on loaded documents: a scalar-form and a ``$ref`` include, quoted\ninclude strings (kept exactly as written), remote in remote (the outermost directive written in a\nlocal file is named) and a remote file included at several places (the one on PATH's walk is\nnamed, else the first in document order).\n\nThe planner and expander tests load real theatres whose remote includes are served by the local\n``remote_server`` fixture, resolve the set with the real TargetResolver and plan it: every edit\ninto remote content (a changed value, a removal across layers, an ``--in-include`` placement, a\ntext-include rewrite, an alias expansion) is refused naming the local include line, while an\noverridden value and a new key below a remote include are planned in the local file.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom remote_support import RemoteServer\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ExitCode, WriteRefusedError\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigDocument, SourceFile\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback import expand as expander\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.aliases import alias_sites\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import plain_equal, root_plain\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.remote_guard import REMOTE_READ_ONLY, remote_refusal\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\n\nROOT = \"troupe.yml\"\n\n\ndef _load(tmp_path: Path, files: Mapping[str, str]) -> ConfigDocument:\n    for name,...[truncated 13607 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/writeback/test_remote_guard.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold1='''    url = remote_server.route(\"/team.yml\", \"a: 1\\\\nb: 2\\\\n\")\n    files = {ROOT: f\"team:\\\\n  $ref: {url}\\\\n  a: 5\\\\n\"}\n\n    error = _refused(tmp_path, files, \"team\", \"{a: 6}\")\n\n    assert str(error) == _message(tmp_path / ROOT, \"team\", url, 2)'''\nnew1='''    url = remote_server.route(\"/team.yml\", \"m:\\\\n  a: 1\\\\n  b: 2\\\\n\")\n    files = {ROOT: f\"team:\\\\n  $ref: {url}\\\\n  m:\\\\n    a: 5\\\\n\"}\n\n    error = _refused(tmp_path, files, \"team.m\", \"{a: 6}\")\n\n    assert str(error) == _message(tmp_path / ROOT, \"team.m\", url, 2)'''\nold2='''    url = remote_server.route(\"/team.yml\", \"a: 1\\\\nb: 2\\\\n\")\n    files = {ROOT: f\"team:\\\\n  $ref: {url}\\\\n  a: 5\\\\n\"}\n\n    assert _plan(tmp_path, files, \"team\", \"{a: 6, b: 2}\") == {\n        ROOT: f\"team:\\\\n  $ref: {url}\\\\n  a: 6\\\\n\"\n    }'''\nnew2='''    url = remote_server.route(\"/team.yml\", \"m:\\\\n  a: 1\\\\n  b: 2\\\\n\")\n    files = {ROOT: f\"team:\\\\n  $ref: {url}\\\\n  m:\\\\n    a: 5\\\\n\"}\n\n    assert _plan(tmp_path, files, \"team.m\", \"{a: 6, b: 2}\") == {\n        ROOT: f\"team:\\\\n  $ref: {url}\\\\n  m:\\\\n    a: 6\\\\n\"\n    }'''\nassert old1 in s and old2 in s\ns=s.replace(old1,new1).replace(old2,new2)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ntimeout 300 uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_remote_guard.py -p no:randomly 2>&1 | tail -12", "description": "Adjust layered tests and rerun"}

The full suite passes (4163 tests), and mypy --strict and ruff are clean.\n\n1. **Messages name sources by `.shown`:** every writer message that names a source file now uses `SourceFile.shown`, so a remote file appears as its exact URL instead of the `http:/host/...` form. That covers the six sites you listed, plus `block_scalar`, `edits`, `verify`, `target`'s root refusal, and the internal layout messages in `layout.py` and `planner.py`. I left `.path` only where the file is always local:\n   - `atomic.py`, which refuses remote sources first;\n   - `SetOutcome.written` and `_restore` in `setter.py`, which only ever hold patched files, and those are local;\n   - `watch.py`, which already filters out remote sources;\n   - `cli/render.py`, which already returns the URL.\n2. **Alias and merge-key guards:** when the alias token or the `<<` key is written in remote content, both guards now raise `remote_refusal`. This applies with and without `--expand-alias`, so the message quotes the local include line and never suggests `--expand-alias`. To give the guards the document they need, `alias_refusal` and `merge_error` now take it as their first argument, and all callers are updated, including the planner's three `merge_error` calls.\n3. **New CLI tests** in `tests/test_set_remote.py`, for both `troupe.yml` and `troupe.yaml`:\n   - an alias inside remote content and a merge-key value inside remote content each exit 3, quote the local include line, don't mention `--expand-alias`, and change no file;\n   - a not-found path below remote content exits 5, stderr starts with the exact URL and never shows `http:/1`.\n\n   I also added unit tests for both guards. One existing unit test changed: the guard now refuses before any expansion, so the expander test builds its alias site directly.\n4. **design.md:** the planner line in the file tree and the "Planner, Shared, Expand" section now say the guard sits at `_Planner._add` and `_replace_file`, with the `_merge_layered` / 6.3 reason. They also record the alias and merge-key routing and the `.shown` rule. I didn't touch the older mentions of `_layout` at design.md line 70 or in research.md Decision 5.\n\nThe new remediation tests were written after the code changes, so there is no captured failing run for them. Reverting `aliases.py` alone to get one isn't possible, because the planner now calls the new `merge_error` signature.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 3.3\n- TASK_BRIEF: Every write into remote content exits 3 naming the local file, PATH, the outermost local include directive exactly as written and its line. Edits are refused at `_Planner._add` / `_replace_file`, alias expansion at the expander, and an alias or merge key written in remote content at the alias and merge-key guards. Overridden remote values and new keys below a remote include are written locally (scalar form converted to `$ref` mapping form, URL unchanged). The include-root refusal names the include string. The temporary 2.4 setter refusal is removed and the atomic backstop kept. Written locations and layout identity use `SourceFile.key`, and every writer message names sources by `SourceFile.shown`.\n- FILES_CHANGED: src/troupe/config/writeback/remote_guard.py (new), src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/expand.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/shared.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/layout.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/aliases.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/target.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/block_scalar.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/edits.py, src/troupe/config/writeback/verify.py, tests/writeback/test_remote_guard.py (new), tests/test_set_remote.py, tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py, spec/spec/config-remote-schema/design.md\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 6.1, 6.2, 6.3, 6.4, 6.5, 6.6, 10.1\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: RemoteGuard (remote_guard.py); Planner, Shared, Expand (updated); Verify, Setter, Atomic, SetCommand; File Structure (planner.py line, updated); Requirements Traceability rows 6.1/6.2, 6.3/6.4, 6.5, 6.6; research.md Decision 5 and choices 5–7\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: Round 1: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_remote_guard.py` before the planner and expander wiring gave 8 failed, 15 passed. I re-ran all round-1 scenarios with the real CLI against a local `RemoteServer`:\n     - **Alias in remote content, with and without `--expand-alias`:** exits 3, names the local file and the include line (line 1), and no longer mentions `--expand-alias`.\n     - **Merge key in remote content:** exits 3 with the same local-include-line message.\n     - **Removal across layers through a `$ref` include:** exits 3 and names the `$ref` line (line 2).\n     - **Not-found paths below remote content** (`s.a.x`, `s.lst.5`): exit 5 and name the exact `http://127.0.0.1:…/s.yml`. The broken `http:/` form is gone.\n     - **Every other round-1 refusal is unchanged:** scalar-form, `$ref` and quoted includes, scalar replaced by a mapping or a list, collection removals, `--in-include`, remote in remote, text include, through a local `file:` include, block scalar, flow entry, list append. All exit 3 with empty stdout and no file changed.\n     - **Successes are unchanged:** a new key converts the include to `$ref` mapping form with the quotes kept; nested new keys and overrides are written in the local file; setting a remote value to its current value is a no-op.\n  2. FYI: The only `.path` uses left in `writeback/` are in `atomic.py`, which keeps refusing remote sources as a backstop, and `SetOutcome.written`. Both only ever see local files.\n  3. Suggestion: design.md lines 69–70 (the "existing architecture" notes) still say "Every edit of a file passes `_Planner._layout(file)`". research.md line 12 says the same. That describes the code as it was before this feature, and both documents now record the `_add`/`_replace_file` decision (design.md around line 402, research.md Decision 5 "Revised during task 3.3"). A short pointer could be added there; it does not block acceptance.\n- REMEDIATION: None required.\n- SUMMARY: Every write into remote content I tried, including alias and merge-key cases, now exits 3 and quotes the local include line exactly as written, while overrides and new keys are written locally; sources are named by their real URL, the guard placement is recorded in the design, and tests, mypy and ruff pass.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Writing include-like VALUE strings and the ``--include`` option (config-remote-schema).\n\n``set`` marks VALUE before target resolution (task 3.5): without ``--include`` every include-like\nstring of VALUE is written tagged ``!literal`` unless the schema gives its own path\n``format: uri``; with ``--include`` it is written plain and loads as an include. ``--include`` is a\n``store_true`` flag under the same rules as ``--in-include`` and ``--expand-alias``. Every test\nruns the real console script against real files and, for remote URLs, the local\n``remote_server`` fixture.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Final\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom remote_support import RemoteServer\nfrom set_support import assert_file_bytes, run_config, snapshot_tree\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config.constants import CONFIG_DIR_NAME, HISTORY_STORE_NAME\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ExitCode\n\nCONFIG_NAMES: Final = [\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"]\n\nURI_SCHEMA: Final = json.dumps({\"properties\": {\"hook\": {\"type\": \"string\", \"format\": \"uri\"}}})\n\"\"\"A schema giving ``hook`` the format ``uri``.\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_config(\"set\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre), cwd=theatre)\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> bytes:\n    result = run_config(\"get\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre), cwd=theatre)\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stderr) == (0, \"\"), result.stderr\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path) -> list[dict[str, object]]:\n    result = run_config(\"history\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre), cwd=theatre)\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stderr) == (0, \"\"), result.stderr\n    return [json.loads(line) for line in result.stdout.decode(\"utf-8\").splitlines()]\n\n\ndef _history_state(theatre: Path) -> d...[truncated 10330 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/writeback/test_value_marks.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=s[s.index(\"def test_unmarked_values_keep_the_file_prefix_rule\"):s.index(\"# --- no-op comparisons\")]\nnew='''def test_parsed_values_are_marked_none_and_stay_plain_until_marked() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 8.1, 10.2: ``parse_value`` marks every scalar ``NONE`` (the design default; the\n    temporary unmarked state is gone), so an include string is a plain value in the plain tree\n    until ``mark_include_strings`` marks it ``INCLUDE``; there is no ``file:`` prefix fallback.\"\"\"\n    path = ConfigPath.parse(\"p\")\n    assert parse_value(\"file:inc.yaml\", path) == NewScalar(\"file:inc.yaml\", None, ScalarMark.NONE)\n    assert new_to_plain(parse_value(\"file:inc.yaml\", path)) == \"file:inc.yaml\"\n    assert new_to_plain(parse_value(\"https://x\", path)) == \"https://x\"\n    ref = new_to_plain(parse_value(\"{$ref: file:inc.yaml, a: 1}\", path))\n    assert ref == {\"$ref\": \"file:inc.yaml\", \"a\": 1}\n\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"ScalarMark\\|_scalar\\b\" tests/writeback/test_value_marks.py | head -5; uv run pytest -n auto -q tests/writeback/test_value_marks.py 2>&1 | tail -3"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\ndef edit(p, pairs):\n    s=open(p).read()\n    for a,b,*n in pairs:\n        assert a in s, (p,a)\n        s=s.replace(a,b) if not n else s.replace(a,b,n[0])\n    open(p,'w').write(s)\nedit('tests/writeback/test_target.py',[\n('''def _resolve(document: ConfigDocument, path: str, value: str) -> SetRequest:\n    config_path = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    return resolve_target(document, config_path, parse_value(value, config_path))''','''def _resolve(\n    document: ConfigDocument, path: str, value: str, *, include: bool = False\n) -> SetRequest:\n    config_path = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    marked = marked_value(document, value, config_path, include=include)\n    return resolve_target(document, config_path, marked)'''),\n('''    \"\"\"Req 2.8: setting an include root to its own ``file:`` string keeps the configuration.\"\"\"\n    document = _load(tmp_path, ROOT_WITH_INCLUDES, INCLUDES)\n    request = _resolve(document, \"agents.coder\", \"file:agents/coder.yaml\")\n    assert plain_equal(request.intended, request.current, ordered=True)''','''    \"\"\"Req 2.8 (config-remote-schema 9.3): setting an include root to its own ``file:`` string\n    with ``--include`` keeps the configuration; assistant: I extended `tests/test_set_schema.py` with end-to-end tests for task 4.3, and all of them pass. Everything runs through the real `troupe config set` against `.troupe/troupe.schema.json`, using a schema that checks every agent through a local `$defs`/`$ref`. I found no product bug, and no product code was changed.\n\nWhat the new tests cover, each for both `troupe.yml` and `troupe.yaml`:\n\n- **Refused sets (exit 3):** a type error at the root and in an included file, an enum error in an included file, a missing required key, an extra key at the root and as an override below an include, a newly written include whose file content is invalid, and an invalid value set through `--expand-alias`. Each asserts empty stdout, the exact stderr line naming the schema file, PATH, the failing key path and the validator's message, every file byte-identical, and no new history event.\n- **Valid sets:** a change inside an included file, a new key written as an override below an include, a set through `--expand-alias`, a new mapping, and a new valid include. Each asserts exit 0, empty output, exactly one file changed with exact bytes, and exactly the expected `set` events.\n- **Already-invalid configuration (exit 4):** a set that fixes nothing, a set of the same value, and a set whose result would be valid all fail at load. Each asserts the exact load-error message, no file changed, and no history file created.\n\n```\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 4.3\n- TASK_BRIEF: E2E through the real CLI with a $defs/$ref schema in .troupe/troupe.schema.json: a set breaking type/enum/required/additionalProperties (root, included file, override below an include, newly written include, --expand-alias) exits 3 with empty stdout, stderr "<schema>: <PATH>: the set would make the configuration invalid: <keypath>: <detail>", files byte-identical, no event; valid sets are written byte-exactly with their set events; a set on an already-invalid configuration exits 4 (load error naming the schema file and key path) with no file or history state created — both troupe.yml and troupe.yaml\n- FILES_CHANGED: tests/test_set_schema.py\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 7.1, 7.2, 5.1, 5.2, 5.6, 10.1\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: none (validation-only task; it relies on schema.validate and writeback/verify.py verify_patch as built in tasks 1.3, 2.2 and 3.4)\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: This task only adds tests for behaviour that already exists, so there was no implementation step. The first run had one false assumption: `uv run pytest tests/test_set_schema.py -q` -> 2 failed, 36 passed. The case setting `agents.coder file:agents/bad.yaml --include` was refused earlier by the include-replacement rule ("<root>/.troupe/agents/coder.yaml: agents.coder: the whole value comes from the include file:agents/coder.yaml; change the included file instead") and never reached the schema check. I changed it to a new key `agents.reviewer`, so the schema check is what refuses it. I did not separately break the product code to watch the new tests fail. Without schema validation, every refusal case would exit 0 instead of 3 with the exact message, and the already-invalid cases would not exit 4.\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest tests/test_set_schema.py -q` -> 40 passed (10 from task 3.4, 30 new). `uv run mypy --strict` -> Success: no issues found in 173 source files. `uv run ruff check .` -> 2 errors, both in tests/test_set_literals.py (E501 line 369, RUF015 line 532). `uv run ruff format --check .` -> 1 file would be reformatted. Both belong to the concurrent task 4.4, not this file.
