assistant: Task 4.1 is done: I added the end-to-end tests to `tests/test_e2e_remote.py` and all 42 tests in that file pass. They exposed no product bugs, so no product code was changed.\n\n**What was added** (the earlier tests from task 2.5 are untouched; I only extended the module docstring):\n- **Reading remote content:** `get`, `show`, `show PATH` and `show --sources` over remote YAML, a `$ref` include with a local override, and remote text. Each is run for both `troupe.yml` and `troupe.yaml`. The tests also check that each URL is fetched once per command and that no file is written apart from history state in `.troupe/`.\n- **Remote inside remote:** both include forms plus text, several levels deep, with `--sources` labels naming the inner URLs.\n- **Literals:** `!literal` strings (plain and quoted, in the local file and in remote content) and `format: uri` strings (at the root, inside remote content, and as list items) print as plain strings. Every one of them points at the local test server, and its log must show only the one real include, so a regression that fetched a literal would fail.\n- **Fetch failures:** 404, refused connection, bad UTF-8, bad YAML, a `file:` include inside remote content, and a remote cycle, each run through `get`, `show` and `history`. Each must exit 4 with empty stdout and one stderr line with the exact expected start (file or URL, key path, URL). No file may change and no history state may be created.\n- **Schema failures:** a remote value that breaks the schema, a root-level failure, bad JSON, an invalid schema document, a different draft named in `$schema`, and a `$ref` that can't be resolved. Each must exit 4 naming the schema file, with no file changed (schema file included) and no history state.\n- **History:** remote leaves are imported. A later remote change is recorded as set/add/remove events by the next command (I used `get`). `history` fails with exit 4 on a 404 without touching the store, and works again once the URL answers.\n- **Watch, using the real `troupe config watch` process:** it never polls the URL. A local edit reloads it, re-fetches, and records the remote change. A failing fetch is reported on stderr and records nothing, and the next local edit is recorded.\n\n**Behaviours worth knowing:**\n- `history` loads the configuration too, so it fails on an unreachable URL. While a fetch is failing, the watch test therefore reads the history store directly instead of calling `history`.\n- When remote YAML is invalid, the error message starts with the URL and has no key path (`<url>: invalid YAML: …`). The requirement only asks for the key path "where known", so this meets it, and the test pins this form.\n\n**Checks run:** `uv run pytest tests/test_e2e_remote.py` (42 passed), `uv run mypy --strict` (no issues), `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .` (both clean). I did not run the full suite and did not commit, as instructed.\n\nThere is no real failing-first output: this task only adds tests, and the product code already passed them. The only failures during writing were two wrong expectations in my own tests, which I corrected.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 4.1\n- TASK_BRIEF: E2E through the real CLI and local remote_server: get/show/show --sources over remote YAML/text, both include forms, remote in remote, troupe.yml/.yaml; !literal and format: uri strings print as strings with no request for them; fetch failures (404, refused, bad UTF-8, bad YAML, file: in remote, cycle) and schema failures exit 4 naming file/key path/URL or schema file with no history state; history imports/reconciles remote leaves and fails on an unreachable URL; watch re-fetches on a local edit and survives a failing fetch.\n- FILES_CHANGED: tests/test_e2e_remote.py\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.5, 1.6, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5, 3.6, 4.1, 4.4, 4.6, 5.2, 5.3, 5.6\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: none (validation-only task; behaviour checked against requirements.md and the tasks.md text of 4.1)\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: Validation task over behaviour that already exists, so no product change and no meaningful RED. The first run `uv run pytest tests/test_e2e_remote.py -q -x` failed on a wrong expectation (list indentation in show output, "At index 46 diff: b' ' != b'-'"), and the watch test failed because `history` itself exits 4 during a 404; both were test-side fixes.\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest tests/test_e2e_remote.py -q` -> 42 passed; `uv run mypy --strict` -> Success: no issues found in 173 source files; `uv run ruff check .` -> All checks passed!; `uv run ruff format --check .` -> 174 files already formatted\n- CONCERNS: (1) The bad-YAML remote error names only the URL, with no key path (`<url>: invalid YAML: ...`); this meets 2.2 ("key path where known") and the test pins it. (3) The tests take about 65 s in total because of real subprocesses and watch timing.\n- EVIDENCE: tests/test_e2e_remote.py: test_get_and_show_print_remote_yaml_and_text_in_both_forms, test_get_and_show_follow_remote_in_remote_in_both_forms, test_literal_and_format_uri_strings_print_as_strings_and_are_never_requested, test_fetch_failure_exits_4_naming_file_key_path_and_url_with_no_history_state (6 failures x get/show/history), test_schema_failure_exits_4_naming_the_schema_file_with_no_history_state (6 cases), test_history_imports_remote_leaves_and_records_a_remote_change_as_reconcile_events, test_history_fails_on_an_unreachable_url_and_keeps_the_history_state, test_watch_command_refetches_on_a_local_edit_and_survives_a_failing_fetch\n```\n\nFiles changed: `/work/repo/tests/test_e2e_remote.py`
assistant: Task 4.4 is ready for review. I added 31 test cases to `tests/test_set_literals.py`, bringing the file to 59, and all 59 pass. I found no product bug and changed no product code. I didn't run the full suite, as you asked.\n\n**What the new tests cover**, all through the real CLI with whole-file byte comparisons:\n- **Quoting styles (8.3):** a tagged string replacing a single-quoted, double-quoted or plain value keeps that style, with comments, blank lines and other entries unchanged. A value with a line break is double-quoted, including over a `|` or `>` block, and is never written as a block. No stray files are left behind.\n- **Nested values (8.1):** each include-like string inside a flow mapping or list is tagged on its own, keys are never tagged, and `show` and `get` print the plain strings.\n- **Schema (8.2):** strings reached through `items` and `additionalProperties` with `format: uri` are written untagged, and a string at a path the schema doesn't mark is still tagged.\n- **Replacing an existing `!literal` (8.4, 8.5):** the tag stays when the new value is include-like and goes away otherwise, an anchor before the tag stays, and history records plain strings. Setting the same string again changes no byte and records no event.\n- **`--expand-alias` (8.4):** the copied mapping keeps `!literal` on its string.\n- **Tags in VALUE (8.7):** they are refused with exit 2 and nothing changes.\n- **Text includes (8.8):** the new content goes into the text file untagged.\n- **`--include` (9.3, 9.4, 9.6, 10.3):**\n  - A remote include is fetched exactly once during the set.\n  - It records one `set` event per leaf, with the reason.\n  - `show` prints the remote content in place.\n  - Local and remote includes in one value are both written plain.\n  - A refused connection, an HTTP 500 or bad YAML gives exit 3, names the file, PATH and URL, and changes nothing.\n\nRemote URLs use only the local test server or `.invalid` hosts. I wrote no failing tests first because this task only checks behaviour that already exists. Instead, two of my own wrong expectations about comment spacing failed on the first run, which shows the byte comparisons catch even a one-space difference.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 4.4\n- TASK_BRIEF: End-to-end CLI tests in tests/test_set_literals.py comparing whole files byte for byte. They cover: include-like VALUE strings tagged `!literal` in plain, single- or double-quoted style and never as a block scalar; nested strings tagged one by one, keys untagged; `format: uri` paths written plain, including via items and additionalProperties; replacing an existing `!literal`, with anchor kept and history of plain strings; no-op on an equal value; `--expand-alias` copy keeps the tag; a tag in VALUE exits 2; text-include content untagged; `--include` local and remote written plain, fetched once, one set event per leaf; an unloadable remote exits 3 with no file changed; `--include` without include strings behaves as without it.\n- FILES_CHANGED: tests/test_set_literals.py\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 8.1, 8.2, 8.3, 8.4, 8.5, 8.6, 8.7, 8.8, 9.1, 9.2, 9.3, 9.4, 9.5, 9.6, 10.1, 10.2, 10.3\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: tasks.md Implementation Notes (3.2: remote URLs only on the local server or `.invalid`); the design's rule that every include string in VALUE is marked before target resolution, the default mark being NONE\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: This task only adds tests for behaviour that already exists, so there is no failing-first run. The first run of `uv run pytest tests/test_set_literals.py` failed on two of my own wrong expectations: `test_tagged_string_keeps_the_style_rules_and_every_other_byte[double-quoted-with-comment]` (actual `b: !literal "file:inc.yaml" # c`, expected two spaces before `#`) and `test_set_over_a_literal_replaces_the_whole_scalar_and_records_the_strings[include-like-stays-tagged]`. Both match config-set-writeback's comment-column rule (a value longer than the old comment column gets one space before the comment), so I corrected the expectations. This shows the byte comparisons catch a one-space difference.\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest tests/test_set_literals.py -q` gives 59 passed; `uv run mypy --strict .` gives Success, no issues in 173 source files; `uv run ruff check .` gives All checks passed; `uv run ruff format --check .` gives 174 files already formatted. Full suite not run, as instructed.\n- CONCERNS: (1) When a tagged value is too long for the old comment gap, the gap shrinks to one space (`a: old  # c` becomes `a: !literal https://… # c`). Untagged long values do the same, so this is existing config-set-writeback behaviour and not a bug. (2) Setting a text include writes the exact string with no trailing newline (`old\n` becomes `https://example.invalid/x`), which is existing config-set-writeback 2.2 behaviour; the test pins it. (3) `--include` positions and combinations, `--include=TEXT`, the `$ref` refusal and the 404 refusal were already in the file from task 3.5 and were not repeated.\n- EVIDENCE: New tests in /work/repo/tests/test_set_literals.py: test_tagged_string_keeps_the_style_rules_and_every_other_byte (7 cases), test_nested_include_strings_are_tagged_one_by_one (3), test_show_prints_written_literals_as_their_strings, test_schema_uri_through_items_and_additional_properties_is_written_plain (3), test_schema_uri_string_is_plain_beside_a_tagged_sibling, test_set_over_a_literal_replaces_the_whole_scalar_and_records_the_strings (4), test_set_of_the_same_literal_string_is_a_no_op (2), test_expand_alias_copy_of_a_literal_anchor_keeps_the_tag, test_value_with_a_literal_tag_is_a_usage_error (2), test_include_like_text_include_content_is_written_untagged, test_set_include_of_a_remote_mapping_fetches_once_and_records_each_leaf, test_set_include_writes_local_and_remote_strings_of_one_value_plain, test_set_include_of_a_remote_url_that_cannot_load_changes_no_file (3: refused, 500, bad-yaml)\n\nFiles changed: /work/repo/tests/test_set_literals.py

tool: Bash {"command": "set -e; rm -rf /tmp/smoke && mkdir -p /tmp/smoke/srv /tmp/smoke/th/.troupe; cd /tmp/smoke/srv\nprintf 'model: opus\\nlimits:\\n  tokens: 100\\n' > team.yml; printf 'hello\\n' > motd.txt\n(python3 -m http.server 8765 --bind 127.0.0.1 >/dev/null 2>&1 &) ; sleep 1\ncat > /tmp/smoke/th/.troupe/troupe.yml <<'EOF'\n# theatre\nteam: http://127.0.0.1:8765/team.yml  # shared\nmotd: http://127.0.0.1:8765/motd.txt\nhook: !literal http://127.0.0.1:8765/nothing.yml\nEOF\ncd /work/repo; export NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1 no_proxy=127.0.0.1\nT=/tmp/smoke/th\nr(){ echo \"\\$ $*\"; uv run troupe config \"$@\" --theatre $T; echo \"[exit $?]\"; }\nset +e\nr get team.model; r show; r show --sources\nr set team.model sonnet\nr set team.extra yes\ncat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nr set webhook https://hooks.invalid/x\nr set notes file:notes.md\ntail -2 $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nprintf '{\"$schema\":\"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\",\"properties\":{\"endpoint\":{\"type\":\"string\",\"format\":\"uri\"},\"team\":{\"type\":\"object\",\"properties\":{\"limits\":{\"properties\":{\"tokens\":{\"type\":\"integer\"}}}}}}}' > $T/.troupe/troupe.schema.json\nr set endpoint https://api.invalid/v1\ntail -1 $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nr set team.limits.tokens lots\nr get endpoint"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/reqscan.py <<'EOF'\nimport ast, re, pathlib, sys\nids = [f\"{r}.{c}\" for r,n in [(1,6),(2,3),(3,6),(4,6),(5,6),(6,6),(7,2),(8,8),(9,6),(10,4)] for c in range(1,n+1)]\nfiles = sys.argv[1:] or [str(p) for p in pathlib.Path(\"tests\").rglob(\"*.py\")]\nfound = {}\nfor f in files:\n    t = ast.parse(pathlib.Path(f).read_text())\n    for node in ast.walk(t):\n        if isinstance(node,(ast.FunctionDef,ast.ClassDef,ast.Module,ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):\n            d = ast.get_docstring(node) or \"\"\n            for m in re.findall(r\"\\b(\\d+\\.\\d+)\\b\", d):\n                found.setdefault(m,set()).add(f)\nprint(\"missing:\", [i for i in ids if i not in found])\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/reqscan.py; for f in tests/test_e2e_remote.py tests/test_set_remote.py tests/test_set_schema.py tests/test_set_literals.py; do echo $f; uv run python /tmp/reqscan.py $f; done"}

assistant: All four tasks are approved. These were test-only changes and I found no blocking issues. All 202 tests in the four files pass, matching the 42 + 61 + 40 + 59 the implementers reported. mypy and ruff are clean.\n\n**Requirement ID coverage:** every ID from 1.1 to 10.4 is named in some test docstring under `tests/`, and also within this feature's own test files alone. Looking at just the four files under review, six IDs aren't named: 4.2, 4.3, 4.5, 5.4, 5.5 and 10.4. All six are covered by the feature's unit and loader tests. Only 4.2 is in one of these tasks' lists (4.1's); see finding 2 under 4.1.\n\n**Hosts:** the tests only use the local `remote_server`, a released port on 127.0.0.1, or `example.invalid`. The only other URLs are `$schema` dialect URIs (2020-12, plus draft-07 to test the refusal), and those are never fetched.\n\n## Review Verdict\n- VERDICT: APPROVED\n- TASK: 4.1\n- MECHANICAL_RESULTS:\n  - Tests: PASS (`uv run pytest -q -n 4` on the 4 files, exit 0, 202 passed)\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS (`uv run mypy --strict` exit 0; `ruff check .` and `ruff format --check .` clean)\n  - Boundary: WITHIN (only tests/test_e2e_remote.py)\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN\n  - RED phase: N/A (validation-only task; no product change)\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. FYI: The tests cover every bullet of the task:\n     - `get`, `show` and `show --sources` over YAML and text includes, in scalar and `$ref` form, including remote in remote, for both `troupe.yml` and `troupe.yaml`. Outputs are compared byte for byte, and the server log is checked for exactly one request per URL.\n     - `!literal` and `format: uri` strings point at the live server, and the test asserts the only request was `/shared.yaml`.\n     - Six fetch failures across get, show and history: exit 4, exact stderr prefix, a single line, no file changed, no history state.\n     - Six schema failures: exit 4, stderr starts with the schema file path.\n     - History import, reconcile and the unreachable-URL case.\n     - The real `troupe config watch` process: no polling, re-fetch on a local edit, and it survives a 404.\n  2. Suggestion: Req 4.2 is in this task's requirement list, but no docstring in tests/test_e2e_remote.py names it. It is already covered end to end in tests/test_e2e_locate_load.py around lines 211-224 (`!literal` on a mapping and before an anchor exits 4), so this does not block.\n  3. FYI: The watch test uses `NOTHING_SECONDS` sleeps and `wait_for_history` / `_wait_until`, the same timing approach as the existing watch tests, so it adds no new flakiness.\n- REMEDIATION: none\n- SUMMARY: The e2e tests prove the remote read paths, literals and schema load failures with byte-exact output, request-log checks and no-state checks, against the local server only.\n\n## Review Verdict\n- VERDICT: APPROVED\n- TASK: 4.2\n- MECHANICAL_RESULTS:\n  - Tests: PASS (`uv run pytest -q -n 4` on the 4 files, exit 0, 202 passed)\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS (mypy --strict, ruff check and ruff format all clean)\n  - Boundary: WITHIN (only tests/test_set_remote.py)\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN\n  - RED phase: N/A\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. FYI: 11 refusal cases × 2 config names cover:\n     - Scalar-form and `$ref`-form values.\n     - A quoted include string with a query and fragment, which proves the include string is reported exactly as written.\n     - Mapping and list replacements, `--in-include`, and remote-in-remote reporting the outermost local include (6.2).\n     - Remote content below a local `file:` include.\n     - Text includes in both forms, and `--expand-alias` in remote content.\n     \n     Each case asserts the whole stderr line (holder file, PATH, include string, line), exit 3, empty stdout, an unchanged tree and unchanged history.\n  2. FYI: The include-root refusals (6.5) assert the full message containing the URL. The success cases (6.3, 6.4) check exact file bytes, including scalar-to-mapping conversion with the URL unchanged, that every other file is unchanged, exactly one `set` event carrying `--reason`, and the values read back with `get`.\n  3. FYI: The expected stderr is built from the product's `REMOTE_READ_ONLY` template. It is still a strong check because the holder, PATH, string and line are all asserted, and earlier tests in this file already follow the same pattern.\n- REMEDIATION: none\n- SUMMARY: Every read-only rule for remote content is pinned with exact stderr, byte-identical trees and no recorded events, and allowed local-override writes are checked byte for byte with their events.\n\n## Review Verdict\n- VERDICT: APPROVED\n- TASK: 4.3\n- MECHANICAL_RESULTS:\n  - Tests: PASS (`uv run pytest -q -n 4` on the 4 files, exit 0, 202 passed)\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS (mypy --strict, ruff check and ruff format all clean)\n  - Boundary: WITHIN (only tests/test_set_schema.py)\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN\n  - RED phase: N/A\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. FYI: The schema checks agents through a local `$defs`/`$ref`. Its 8 refusal cases cover type, enum, required, `additionalProperties: false` at the root and below an include, a new invalid include, and `--expand-alias`. Each asserts the full stderr (schema file, PATH, failing key path, detail), exit 3, an unchanged tree and unchanged history.\n  2. FYI: The 5 valid sets check the changed file byte for byte, that every other file is unchanged, and the exact `set` events. The already-invalid configuration exits 4 with the exact message and no history state, including a set whose result would be valid.\n  3. Suggestion (cosmetic): the docstring of `test_set_keeping_a_ref_schema_valid_is_written_byte_exactly` has an odd line break ("exits\n    0").\n- REMEDIATION: none\n- SUMMARY: The schema refusals and valid writes are proven end to end with exact messages, bytes and events, including the exit-4 case for an already-invalid configuration.\n\n## Review Verdict\n- VERDICT: APPROVED\n- TASK: 4.4\n- MECHANICAL_RESULTS:\n  - Tests: PASS (`uv run pytest -q -n 4` on the 4 files, exit 0, 202 passed)\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS (mypy --strict, ruff check and ruff format all clean)\n  - Boundary: WITHIN (only tests/test_set_literals.py)\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN\n  - RED phase: N/A\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. FYI: The new tests cover:\n     - Quoting styles, comment columns and line breaks (always double-quoted, never a block scalar) — 8.3.\n     - Nested flow VALUEs tagged one string at a time, with keys never tagged — 8.1.\n     - `format: uri` reached through `items`, `additionalProperties` and a sibling key — 8.2.\n     - Replacing an existing `!literal`, with an anchor before the tag and with the tag dropped for non-include strings, plus history values — 8.4, 9.6.\n     - Same-string no-ops — 8.5.\n     - The `--expand-alias` copy keeping the tag.\n     - Tagged VALUEs exiting 2 — 8.7.\n     - Text-include content written untagged — 8.8.\n     - `--include` with remote and mixed local and remote strings, one request, one event per leaf — 9.3, 9.6, 10.3.\n     - An unloadable remote `--include` (refused, 500, bad YAML) exiting 3 with the tree and history state unchanged — 9.4.\n     \n     The tests already in the file cover the `hook: !literal https://x` basic case, the `$ref` refusal without `--include` (8.6), `--include` without include strings (9.5), flag positions (9.1) and `--include=TEXT` (9.2). Every file is compared byte for byte with `assert_file_bytes`.\n  2. FYI: All literal URLs use `example.invalid`. Includes that really load go to the local server or to a closed local server.\n- REMEDIATION: none\n- SUMMARY: Writing include-like VALUEs and `--include` is covered end to end, byte for byte, across every 8.x and 9.x criterion, with no external hosts.

tool: Agent {"description": "Validate config-remote-schema impl", "prompt": "In /work/repo, run the feature-level validation for spec `config-remote-schema` by reading and following /work/repo/.claude/skills/method-command/SKILL.md (feature = config-remote-schema). All tasks in spec/spec/config-remote-schema/tasks.md are marked done and committed on main. Facts already established (don't redo unless you doubt them): the full suite `uv run pytest -n auto -q` passed at the current code (4347 passed, ~10.5 min) — do NOT re-run the full suite; mypy --strict and ruff are clean; every requirement ID is named by a test docstring. Focus on cross-task consistency and spec coverage against the verbatim contract below, by reading code and running targeted tests and real-CLI spot checks (use a throwaway `python3 -m http.server` on 127.0.0.1 or the tests' RemoteServer; set NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1; never real internet hosts; work in /tmp and do not modify repository files).\n\nContract (verbatim):\n- A string starting `https://` or `http://` is also an include; it is fetched when the configuration loads (YAML if the URL path ends `.yml`/`.yaml`, otherwise text). A failed fetch → exit 4.\n- Changing a value that comes from a remote include → exit 3; stderr contains the include string exactly as written in the local file. (New keys under a remote include become local overrides, per step 5.)\n- `<dir>/.troupe/troupe.schema.json` (JSON Schema 2020-12), if present, describes the configuration. A string whose schema — found through `properties`, `items` and `additionalProperties` — has `\"format\": \"uri\"` is a literal, not an include. A string tagged `!literal` is a literal.\n- The loaded configuration must validate against the schema, else exit 4. A `set` that would make it invalid → exit 3.\n- `set PATH VALUE` where `VALUE` is a string that would be read as an include and the schema does not mark `PATH` as `format: uri` writes it tagged `!literal`, unless `--include` is given.\nGeneral conventions:...[truncated 388 chars]

Code: src/troupe/config/ (includes.py, fetch.py, schema.py, loader.py, model.py, errors.py, watch.py) and src/troupe/config/writeback/ (remote_guard.py, value.py, layout.py, shared.py, planner.py, compare.py, verify.py, setter.py) and src/troupe/cli/set_command.py.\nCheck and report concisely (file:line evidence, <50 lines):\n1. assistant: The implementation matches the spec on all seven points. The only discrepancy is a wording conflict in the spec itself about one write check in `atomic.py` (point 4). Everything was checked read-only with grep and git; no tests were run.\n\n**1. File Structure Plan vs layout.** Every file the plan lists is present: the 3 new core files, `writeback/remote_guard.py`, and all the modified core, writeback and cli files, plus the test files.\n- Five writeback files changed that the plan doesn't list: `aliases.py`, `block_scalar.py`, `collection_render.py`, `edits.py` and `target.py`.\n- Those diffs are small. Most change error messages from `file.path` to `.shown` (e.g. `block_scalar.py`, `edits.py`, `target.py`). `collection_render.py` keeps `!literal` strings on their own line. They follow from the "names SourceFile.shown" and 8.3 commitments, so this is minor plan drift rather than a boundary breach.\n\n**2. Dependency direction.** It is clean.\n- No core module in `src/troupe/config/*.py` imports `writeback` or `cli`. The only hits are docstrings, at `yaml_style.py:4` and `scalars.py:14`.\n- `fetch.py` is imported only by `loader.py:112`.\n- Network code (`urllib`, `http.client`, `socket`) appears only in `fetch.py:9-12`. `includes.py:11` imports `urllib.parse.urlsplit`, which only parses strings.\n- `value.py` and `remote_guard.py` import only core modules, as the design requires.\n\n**3. Leftover duplication.** No consolidation was missed.\n- Nothing in src builds an include string as `"file:" + ...`. The only place that does it is `IncludeRef.string` (`model.py:200`).\n- The `INCLUDE_PREFIX` uses are all legitimate:\n  - `includes.py` defines and checks the prefixes.\n  - `loader.py:157` builds the list of forms for an error message.\n  - `loader.py:247` (`_spec`) is the inverse of `IncludeRef.string` and keeps URLs whole.\n- Every other `startswith(` hit is lexical text scanning (BOM, quotes, `-`, `,`, `!`), not include detection.\n- Writeback uses `include.string` at `shared.py:530`, `planner.py:738` and `compare.py:92`, and `is_include_string` at `value.py:103`.\n- The 2.3 note is followed:\n  - `shared.written_location` now uses `origin.file.key` (`shared.py:108-110`).\n  - `layout.py` compares by key at lines 422 and 764, and has no `real_path` left.\n  - The remaining `real_path` uses are in `atomic.py:45` (after the remote check), `loader.py:268-304` (local file paths only; remote keys use the URL, line 717) and `lock.py`. None of them can see a remote source.\n\n**4. Temporary refusal from 2.4.** The set-wide refusal is fully removed.\n- Commit 57a829a added `REMOTE_SETS_REFUSED` and `_refuse_remote` in `setter.py`. Commit 7ab274b deleted both, and grep finds no trace in src or tests.\n- `atomic.py:25,37-38` still refuses with `REMOTE_NEVER_WRITTEN` when `file.url` is set. The design's File Structure Plan keeps this as a backstop ("atomic.py # backstop: never write a remote source"). But tasks.md "Safety window" says 3.3 "removes both together".\n- So the spec contradicts itself: the code follows design.md, and the tasks.md text is stale. The check is reachable code, not dead code, but no test pins `REMOTE_NEVER_WRITTEN`.\n\n**5. jsonschema.** It is used only in `schema.py`.\n- `validate` builds `Draft202012Validator(contents, registry=Registry())` at `schema.py:133`, so no remote `$ref` is fetched. An unresolvable `$ref` becomes a `ConfigLoadError` (lines 136-137).\n- There is no `FormatChecker` anywhere, so `format` is only an annotation (docstring at lines 11-12).\n- The only caller of `validate` is `loader.py:213`. Read-back in `verify.py` reaches it through the loader.\n\n**6. TODO/FIXME/TBD/XXX/HACK and secrets.** There are none in `src/troupe`, and no hardcoded secrets. The only `token` hits are variable names for YAML/alias tokens.\n\n**7. Orphaned code.** None. I checked every new `def`/`class`/`Final` constant in `git diff 537bd05^ HEAD -- src`, and each has at least one reference besides its definition. The least-used, such as `read_remote`, `_ordered_refs`, `_final_patches`, `SET_MAKES_INVALID` and `REF_MAPPING_NEEDS_INCLUDE`, each have exactly one call site.

assistant: Step 7 is done and committed on `main` (last commit `e6c8a62`), with nothing pushed and no PR. The full suite passes (4347 tests), and `mypy --strict` and ruff are clean. Final validation returned GO, and I also ran your scenarios through the real CLI against a local HTTP server.\n\n**What works now**\n- **Remote includes:** an `https://` or `http://` string is fetched when the configuration loads, as YAML if the URL path ends `.yml`/`.yaml` and as text otherwise. A failed fetch exits 4, naming the local file, the key path and the URL. `show --sources` labels remote values with their URL.\n- **Remote content is read-only:** changing a remote value exits 3, and the message quotes the include line from the local file, for example: `troupe.yml: team.model: the value comes from the remote include http://…/team.yml (line 2); troupe does not change remote content: change that include line or the shared configuration`. New keys under a remote include become local overrides, so `team: <url>` becomes `team:` / `$ref: <url>` / `newkey: …`.\n- **Schema:**\n  - If `.troupe/troupe.schema.json` is present, the configuration must validate against it, else exit 4.\n  - A `set` that would make it invalid exits 3.\n  - Strings marked `"format": "uri"` (found through `properties`, `items` and `additionalProperties`) and strings tagged `!literal` are values, never includes. Every other explicit tag still exits 4.\n- **Writing URLs and `file:` strings:** `set hook https://…` writes `hook: !literal https://…`, unless the schema marks that path as a URI or `--include` is given.\n\n**Choices I made where the contract was silent** (all recorded in the spec's `research.md`; say if you want any changed):\n- A `file:` include inside remote content exits 4, because there is no local folder to resolve it against.\n- `!literal` is allowed only on single values. It exits 4 on a mapping, a list or a key, or when written before an anchor (`!literal &a x`).\n- Include-like strings nested inside a mapping or list VALUE are also written `!literal`. A `{$ref: …}` VALUE without `--include` exits 3.\n- Remote content is not cached on disk. `watch` never polls URLs, but it re-fetches them on every reload and does watch the schema file.\n- `history` also fetches remote includes and checks the schema, since it loads the configuration on every run.\n- Fetches time out after 10 seconds. Schema `$ref`s are never fetched over the network.\n- Earlier `file:` VALUEs were written plain and became includes. They are now written `!literal` unless `--include` is given, and the older tests were updated to match.\n\n**Two things you might trip over**\n- The library behind the schema check, `jsonschema`, is now a dependency. Out of the box it downloads a schema's remote `$ref`, so the validator is set up to never do that.\n- Replacing a whole remote include (`set team foo`) still uses the older "change the included file instead" refusal. It exits 3 and contains the URL, but it doesn't give the local line number like the other remote refusals do.\n\nBefore starting, I also committed some leftover step 6 changes that were sitting uncommitted (spec notes and roadmap ticks), after checking that the suite passed with them.
